# C1. Efficient architectures for VLA policies and emerging designs

733 items, sorted by relevance then year.

## Cluster summary


**Records:** 287 in `C1.jsonl` (286 verified, 1 unverified). **Searches:** 88 (see `C1_searchlog.md`).
Relevance split: 52 rated 5, 118 rated 4, 96 rated 3, 21 rated 2.
**Caveats:**
- Semantic Scholar was rate-limited for the whole run, so citation-graph snowballing was replaced by seed-name and keyword searches.
- The saturation rule was not met. The last 10 searches still produced 14 new items, most of them 2026 preprints.

### Technique families

1. **Small / lightweight VLAs (backbone downsizing).**
   - Seeds: TinyVLA, MiniVLA (~1B, Qwen2.5-0.5B), SmolVLA (~450M, trained on community data), EdgeVLA.
   - Also: FLOWER (950M, pruned VLM layers), Evo-1 (0.77B), VLA-Adapter (0.5B backbone), NanoVLA (Jetson Orin Nano), XS-VLA (0.25B).
   - A newer wave drops the LLM entirely and fuses separately encoded vision and language into a compact head: TurboVLA (0.2B, ~32 Hz on an RTX 4090, <1 GB VRAM) and DEM (6.1 ms per forward pass).
   - SSM backbones: RoboMamba, AnoleVLA, and a Mamba action expert for SmolVLA.
   - Hypernetworks that activate only a small task-specific policy: HyperVLA (90x fewer active parameters), TeNet.
2. **Action representation and tokenization.**
   - Seed tokenizers: FAST (DCT+BPE) and FAST+.
   - Learned or structured tokenizers: VQ-VLA, BEAST (B-spline), OmniSAT, FASTer, OAT (ordered, anytime decoding), ActionCodec, X-Tokenizer.
   - Continuous parameterizations: Legendre polynomials (FLASH) and B-splines (ABPolicy).
3. **Parallel and non-autoregressive decoding.**
   - OpenVLA-OFT (parallel decoding + chunking + L1 regression; 26x throughput).
   - Jacobi-style decoding: PD-VLA, CEED-VLA.
   - Discrete and block diffusion: Discrete Diffusion VLA, BlockVLA, TBD-VLA, dVLA, Holo-M.
   - Speculative decoding: Spec-VLA, KERV, HeiSD, WA-SpecDec, FLASH-spec, TS-DP.
4. **Few-step and one-step generative action heads.**
   - Distillation: Consistency Policy, OneDP (1.5 to 62 Hz), ManiCM, SnapFlow (pi0.5 latency 274 to 83 ms), VDD (distilled into a mixture of experts).
   - MeanFlow and shortcut models: MeanFlow VLA, HybridFlow (2 NFE, Jetson Thor), ElasticFlow, ReactVLA, MVP, DriftingVLA.
   - Straighter paths: optimal-transport couplings, WarmPrior, CF-VLA (learned coarse initialization), normalizing-flow heads (NinA).
   - Evidence that heavy iteration may be unnecessary: "Much Ado About Noising" (a two-step regression policy matches flow policies) and "Let It Be Simple" (one-step decoding works because of the condition-target structure).
5. **Dynamic / adaptive compute.**
   - Early exit: DeeR-VLA, decoupled early exits for flow VLAs, LoopVLA (recurrent depth).
   - Layer skipping and mixtures of layers: MoLe-VLA, DySL-VLA, ActDistill, AC²-VLA.
   - Adaptive denoising steps: D3P, FASA, AdaVLA, ELASTIC.
   - MoE: MoDE (90% fewer FLOPs via expert caching), AdaMoE, Dense-to-MoE deactivation, GeRM.
6. **Visual token pruning, merging and compression.** VLA-Pruner, LightVLA (differentiable), SP-VLA, ADP, BFA++, SAFE-Pruner, CogVLA, SemanticVLA, Compressor-VLA, GridS, ST-Merge (8.3x at high resolution), DepthCache, TIES.
7. **Temporal reuse: KV, feature and action caching.**
   - Token and KV reuse: VLA-Cache (seed), TVCache, learned caching, DySta, Gated VLA-Cache, Reflex (streaming KV).
   - Denoiser feature caching: EfficientVLA, EVO cache schedules, test-time sparsity.
   - Action reuse: ActionCache (warm starts), FlashVLA (action reuse), rMuscle (reuse across repeated executions).
   - A warning: training-free token skipping can compound errors across control steps unless it is periodically refreshed with a dense pass ("The Gate, Not the Cache"). Prefix caching also showed limited gains in the CrossVLA study.
8. **Action chunking and real-time execution.**
   - Foundations: ACT and temporal ensembling, Diffusion Policy with receding horizon, BID.
   - Asynchronous execution: RTC (inference-time inpainting), training-time RTC, Legato, REMAC, VLASH (roll the state forward), A2C2 (residual correction), FASTER (time-to-first-action), urgency-aware denoising, Streaming Diffusion Policy, FlashVLA streaming, RAVEL.
   - Adaptive horizons: AAC, GeoAAC, PACE, Mixture of Horizons, PolicyTrim.
   - Practical stacks: LeRobot async inference, Dexmal real-time pi0 at 30 Hz, Jetson-PI (8.66x control-rate gain on Orin).
9. **Dual-system / fast-slow / hierarchical designs.**
   - Seeds: Helix (7B VLM at 7-9 Hz plus an 80M model at 200 Hz, onboard), GR00T N1/N1.5/N1.6, Hi Robot, pi0.5.
   - Research systems: RoboDual, HiRT, Fast-in-Slow (117.7 Hz), DuoCore-FS, UniFS (multi-rate layers), Latent Bridge (50-75% fewer VLM calls), TIDAL, StreamVLA, EMS model switching, SkipVLA (classical planner for free-space motion).
   - Cloud-edge splits: CloudEdgeVLA, AsyncVLA-nav, VLA-ULAP.
10. **Efficient reasoning VLAs.** Latent CoT and early exit: Fast-ThinkAct, AVA-VLA, LaRA-VLA, LaST0. Reasoning reuse: Fast ECoT. Parallel CoT: DualCoT-VLA.
11. **Measurement and systems characterization** (overlaps with C3 and C9).
   - VLA-Perf analytical model, cross-XPU CET leaderboard, and cross-platform edge/cloud scaling.
   - Jetson Orin/Thor characterization: the action phase is memory-bound and can be up to 75% of latency.
   - Embodied-efficiency metrics and the "speedup paradox": per-step speedups do not always shorten tasks.
   - Runtimes: vla.cpp, Embodied.cpp, vla.simd (CPU / Raspberry Pi 5).

### Key open problems

- **Metric incomparability.** Papers report relative latency, open-loop chunk rate, closed-loop control rate, FLOPs or throughput, often on different GPUs. Task-level completion time and energy are rarely reported (2603.19131 and 2606.28529 show why this matters). A shared on-robot, latency-paired benchmark is missing.
- **Speed versus reactivity.** Chunking and async execution hide latency but create stale-observation and chunk-boundary problems. There is no consensus method: inference-time RTC, training-time RTC, VLASH and A2C2 each win in different delay regimes (2605.08168).
- **Compounding errors of training-free acceleration** such as token skipping, caching and pruning under closed-loop rollout. Most papers evaluate only on LIBERO or SIMPLER, where success rates are near saturation.
- **Keeping VLM knowledge while shrinking or insulating the backbone.** See knowledge insulation, VLM4VLA, and the finding that LLM-free designs nearly match large VLAs on standard benchmarks. It is unclear whether generality survives in the open world.
- **Few-step and one-step generation.** When is it lossless (condition-target structure, multimodality)? How does it interact with RL fine-tuning and test-time scaling?
- **Onboard deployment evidence is thin.** Few works report numbers on Jetson, NPU or CPU hardware. Industrial systems such as Helix, Gemini Robotics On-Device and DYNA-1 disclose little technical detail.
- **Composing techniques.** How do quantization, pruning, caching, few-step sampling and async execution stack together? How should compute be allocated across the backbone, action expert and denoising steps (2609.29382)?

### 10 most important works (for this workshop's framing)

1. **pi0 / pi0.5 + FAST + Knowledge Insulation + RTC** (Physical Intelligence; 2410.24164, 2504.16054, 2501.09747, 2505.23705, 2506.07339): the flow-action-expert template, its tokenizer, training recipe and real-time execution method.
2. **OpenVLA-OFT** (2502.19645): parallel decoding + chunking + continuous actions give 26x throughput. This is the efficiency baseline recipe.
3. **Helix** (Figure, 2025 blog): the reference industrial onboard fast-slow design (200 Hz S1 / 7-9 Hz S2).
4. **GR00T N1** (2503.14734): open dual-system humanoid VLA and a common acceleration target.
5. **SmolVLA** (2506.01844) with LeRobot async inference: small VLA plus an open async stack.
6. **Diffusion Policy → Consistency Policy / OneDP** (2303.04137, 2405.07503, 2410.21257): the line of work taking diffusion policies to few or one sampling steps.
7. **VLA-Cache** (2502.02175) and **EfficientVLA** (2506.10100): training-free temporal and structural redundancy removal.
8. **DeeR-VLA** (2411.02359): dynamic early exit under compute and memory budgets.
9. **VLASH** (2512.01031) / **Running VLAs at Real-time Speed** (2510.26742) / **Jetson-PI** (2607.12659): practical real-time and onboard inference.
10. **VLA-Perf** (2602.18397) together with embodied-efficiency metrics (2603.19131): how to reason about and measure VLA latency on real systems.

## Annotated bibliography


### Relevance 5

- **[A Motion-Aware Vector Quantization Framework with Centroid Reuse for Efficient VLA Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24148)** — Zhuoran Song, Haozhe Jiang, Chunyu Qi, Minnan Pei et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: vector quantization, hardware co-design, motion-aware vector quantization, codebook-domain GEMM, custom accelerator, centroid reuse; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: VQVLA quantizes VLA weights into codebooks whose precision adapts to robot motion state and builds an accelerator that computes directly on centroid indices with reuse.
  - Efficiency: 6.5x speedup over A100 | 6.5x over A100, 2.8x over Dadu-Corki, 1.9x over LUT-DLA (relative latency) on custom accelerator (vs A100)
- **[Action Chunk Scheduling for Batched Robot Policy Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00337)** — Rohan Bansal, David He, Nadun Ranawaka Arachchige, Zhenyang Chen et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: serving scheduling, batching, chunk-aware scheduling, chunk-aware batch scheduling, remote GPU serving, chunk-rate-aware scheduling; clusters: C1, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7
  - TL;DR: Formulates serving one VLA to many robots from a remote GPU as a scheduling problem and shows chunk-consumption-aware scheduling beats conventional batching when robots consume chunks at different rates.
  - Efficiency: up to 18% higher system throughput | up to 18% system throughput improvement in real-world experiments (throughput)
- **[ActionCache: Training-Free Acceleration for Vision-Language-Action Models with Action Caching and Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06370)** — Ryuji Oi, Hikari Otsuka, Kosuke Matsushima, Yuki Ichikawa et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: action caching, warm-start denoising, action caching and refinement, training-free; clusters: C1, C3, C9
  - TL;DR: Stores intermediate actions under compact multimodal keys and warm-starts flow denoising from retrieved similar contexts.
  - Efficiency: action-head speedup up to 10.44x (pi0.5) and 40.17x (GR00T N1.6) (relative latency)
- **[ActQuant: Sub-4-bit Action-Guided Quantization for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24011)** — Arash Akbari, Arman Akbari, Masih Eskandar, Qitao Tan et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: mixed-precision post-training quantization, action-guided mixed-precision quantization, low-bit kernels, OmniModel.cpp runtime, action-guided quantization; clusters: C1, C2, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Allocates bit-widths per weight matrix by action sensitivity and tunes block scales, then ships models through a native C++ low-bit runtime; retains accuracy near 3 bits per weight.
  - Efficiency: 95.0% retained at <=3 bpw (OpenVLA-OFT); backbone 14.3 GB -> 2.7 GB at 2.5 bpw (relative memory)
- **[AHA-WAM:Asynchronous Horizon-Adaptive World-Action Modeling with Observation-Guided Context Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09811)** — Jisong Cai, Long Ling, Shiwei Chu, Zhongshan Liu et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: low-frequency video DiT planner with rolling KV memory, high-frequency action DiT, observation-guided context routing, decoupled temporal resolutions, async; clusters: C1, C6, C7, C8, C9
  - TL;DR: Decouples the video world branch (slow, long-horizon context) from a fast action DiT that queries cached video context each step, so actions run at ~24 Hz without rerunning the video model.
  - Efficiency: 24.17 Hz closed-loop control; 4.59x speedup over Fast-WAM (closed-loop control)
- **[Algorithm-Architecture Co-Design for Efficient VLA Inference via Speculative Inference and Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15636)** — Chunyu Qi, Zhuoran Song, Jian Weng, Haozhe Jiang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: speculative inference, mixed-precision quantization, hardware co-design, speculative execution, state-aware speculative long-horizon prediction, small verification VLA, block-wise mixed-precision quantization, heterogeneous architecture, speculative inference and verification; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: SpecVLA predicts long action sequences speculatively in low-stakes states and verifies them selectively with a small quantized model, running on a GPU plus a robot-specific hardware module.
- **[AsyncVLA: An Asynchronous VLA for Fast and Robust Navigation on the Edge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13476)** — Noriaki Hirose, Catherine Glossop, Dhruv Shah, Sergey Levine · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: cloud-edge split, asynchronous execution, hierarchical remote/onboard architecture, edge adapter, asynchronous inference, hierarchical async control, onboard edge adapter, edge-cloud split, lightweight onboard adapter, trajectory re-weighting; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C5, C6, C7, C9

- **[AURA: Action-Gated Memory for Robot Policies at Constant VRAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02775)** — Josef Chen, J. Chen · 2026 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: recurrent memory, learned write gating, gated recurrent memory, write reduction; clusters: C1, C2, C5
  - TL;DR: Replaces a growing KV cache with a fixed-size recurrent memory that only writes when the observation would change the next action, keeping long-episode memory constant on edge devices without hurting OpenVLA-OFT success.

Papers: 4 pages, non-archival, via OpenReview, due Oct 12.
- **[Efficient-WAM: A 1B-Parameter World-Action Model with Low-Cost Future Imagination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10040)** — Jiajun Li, Tiecheng Guo, Yifan Ye, Rongyu Zhang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: compact video expert transfer, token sparsity, fewer video than action denoising steps, token-sparse video latents, fewer video steps than action steps; clusters: C1, C3, C7, C8, C9
  - TL;DR: A 1B WAM that treats future video as coarse guidance rather than a photoreal target, using token-sparse latents and asymmetric denoising to reach ~98 ms per chunk with success comparable to much larger WAMs.
  - Efficiency: 98 ms per action chunk in physical deployment; >30x faster than Motus (open-loop chunk rate)
- **[Embodied Efficiency Challenge / embodied-efficiency-bench (CoRL 2026 Workshop: Efficient Foundation Models for Real-Time Embodied AI)](https://efficient-embodied-ai.github.io/)** — Workshop organizers · 2026 · CoRL 2026 Workshop · benchmark
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: quantization, sharding, offload; clusters: C1, C4, C5
  - TL;DR: Challenge compressing OpenVLA-7B to fit Jetson Orin NX 16GB with a frozen Docker harness; ranked by success within 15 points of reference, then median and p99 latency per action, then params and peak memory, with organizer re-runs of top entries.
- **[Embodied.cpp: A Portable Inference Runtime of Embodied AI Models on Heterogeneous Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02501)** — Ling Xu, Borui Li, Hao Wu, Chuyu Han et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: inference runtime, quantized inference runtime, kernel fusion, fused inference, multi-rate execution, portable inference runtime, fused batch-1 inference, quantization, portable C++ inference runtime, latency-first fused inference, portable runtime; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C8, C9
  - TL;DR: Portable C++ runtime for VLAs and WAMs organized in five layers, targeting the embodied runtime contract of multi-rate closed-loop execution and latency-first batch-1 inference.
  - Efficiency: 1.05-2.70x speedup, 7-77% lower VRAM vs Python | 1.05-2.70x speedup and 7-77% lower VRAM vs Python baselines (relative latency) on heterogeneous edge devices
  - Code: https://github.com/SEU-PAISys/Embodied.cpp
- **[Fast-WAM: Do World Action Models Need Test-time Future Imagination?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16666)** — Tianyuan Yuan, Zibin Dong, Yicheng Liu, Hang Zhao · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: single-pass video DiT encoder, video co-training only at train time, skip future prediction at inference; clusters: C1, C7, C8, C9
  - TL;DR: Shows WAMs keep most of their benefit if video prediction is used only as a training signal: the video DiT runs once as an encoder at test time, cutting latency to 190 ms (4x faster) with competitive success.
  - Efficiency: 190 ms latency; >4x faster than imagine-then-execute WAMs (relative latency)
  - Code: https://yuantianyuan01.github.io/FastWAM/
- **[Faster and Better? Benchmark Bugs and Design Limitations Distort the Evaluation of Vision-Language-Action Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37771)** — Qiwei Chen, Kaijun Zhou, Nuohui Shi, Zhiyang Li et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: training-free acceleration, token pruning, caching, benchmark audit; clusters: C1, C4, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Audits why training-free VLA accelerators sometimes beat the uncompressed baseline: finds 22 bugs and 4 design limitations across seven benchmarks (permissive checkers, wrong masses), and fixing them can reverse method rankings.
- **[Faster-WAM: Do World Action Models Need Deep Action Modules?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02365)** — Liheng Ma, Rui Heng Yang, Zhanguang Zhang, Mateo Clemente et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: single-layer action head docked to video backbone, multi-layer KV fusion, RoPE realignment; clusters: C1, C8
  - TL;DR: Replaces deep action towers in WAMs with a one-layer action head that reads keys/values from all layers of a 30-layer video transformer, giving the lowest latency in a controlled comparison without losing accuracy.
  - Efficiency: 66.5 ms per inference; 3.2x faster than Fast-WAM (relative latency)
- **[FASTER: Rethinking Real-Time Flow VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19199)** — Yuxiang Lu, Zhe Liu, Xianzhe Fan, Zhenya Yang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: non-uniform denoising schedule, streaming pipeline, horizon-aware flow schedule, streaming client-server, streaming client-server pipeline, asynchronous inference, non-uniform flow schedule, reaction-aware async; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Analyzes reaction latency of chunked flow VLAs as governed by time-to-first-action and execution horizon, then uses a horizon-aware sampling schedule that finishes near-term actions in one step while refining later ones, cutting effective reaction latency.
  - Efficiency: immediate-reaction denoising compressed ~10x into a single step (relative latency) on consumer-grade GPUs
- **[Finetuning Vision-Language-Action Models Requires Fewer Layers Than You Think](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20246)** — Gia-Binh Nguyen, Trong-Bao Ho, Thien-Loc Ha, Khoa Vo et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: layer pruning, training-free compression; clusters: C1, C2, C3
  - TL;DR: Finds redundant 'twin' layers in flow-matching VLAs via a single-pass CKA analysis and removes them before fine-tuning, speeding both training and inference.
  - Efficiency: up to 50% depth reduction; up to 30% faster inference (relative latency)
- **[FoldQuantVLA: Native Low-Bit Quantization of Vision-Language-Action Models via Consistent Folding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24433)** — Hung T. Ho, Khanh-Binh Nguyen, Quan Nguyen, Thanh Duong et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: post-training quantization, integer kernels, Hadamard transform, custom TensorRT plugins, mixed precision, channel scaling, block Hadamard; clusters: C1, C2, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: PTQ with channel scaling, block Hadamard and dynamic per-token quantization executed natively as W4A4 TensorRT plugins on Orin; mixed W8A8 for sensitive projections boosts real-robot success.
  - Efficiency: 1.20-1.33x speedup over FP TensorRT on Orin; 1.25-1.52x on desktop (relative latency) on Jetson AGX Orin; Ada GPUs
  - Code: https://github.com/cair-vinuni/FoldQuantVLA
- **[From Inference Efficiency to Embodied Efficiency: Revisiting Efficiency Metrics for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19131)** — Zhuofan Li, Hongkun Yang, Zhenyang Chen, Yangxuan Chen et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: evaluation methodology, evaluation of compression, token sparsification, action compression, model compression, token sparsification, action sequence compression; clusters: C1, C2, C4, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Argues FLOPs/parameters/token throughput misrepresent VLA efficiency on robots and proposes system-level metrics such as completion time, smoothness and motion energy; compression often worsens them.
- **[Habilis-$β$: A Fast-Motion and Long-Lasting On-Device Vision-Language-Action Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18813)** — Tommoro Robotics, :, Jesoon Kang, Taegeon Park et al. · 2026 · arXiv · tech-report
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: rectified-flow distillation, motion shaping, phase-adaptive motion shaping, classifier-free guidance; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5
  - TL;DR: Tommoro Robotics' on-device VLA uses flow distillation for high-frequency edge control and proposes productivity metrics (tasks/hour, time between interventions) for continuous operation.
  - Efficiency: real humanoid logistics: 124 TPH, 137.4 s MTBI (vs 19 TPH for pi0.5) (other)
- **[HBVLA: Pushing 1-Bit Post-Training Quantization for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13710)** — Xin Yan, Zheng-Lin Wan, Feiyang Ye, Xing-Rui Yu et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: 1-bit post-training quantization, Hessian saliency, binarization; clusters: C1, C2, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: VLA-specific binarization: policy-aware Hessian picks salient weights, sparse orthogonal transform eases the rest, then group-wise 1-bit quantization in the Haar domain.
  - Efficiency: 92.2% of FP performance (OpenVLA-OFT, LIBERO); 93.6% (CogACT, SimplerEnv) (other)
- **[HoloQ-VLA: Uniform W4A4 Quantization of Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28803)** — Xin-Yu Wang, Ming-Ze Li, Sicheng Lyu, Dong-Xiu Liu et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: post-training quantization, rotation, rotation + Hadamard PTQ, per-step scaling, W4A4 PTQ; clusters: C1, C2, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Training-free PTQ that quantizes both the language backbone and the diffusion action head of VLAs uniformly to W4A4 for on-device deployment.
  - Efficiency: 74.2% static memory reduction at W4A4 (relative memory)
- **[How Fast Can I Run My VLA? Demystifying VLA Inference Performance with VLA-Perf](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18397)** — Wenqi Jiang, Jason Clemons, Karu Sankaralingam, Christos Kozyrakis et al. · 2026 · arXiv · benchmark
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: analytical performance modeling, performance modeling, asynchronous inference, dual-system pipelines, analytical performance modelling, deployment placement analysis, analytical roofline-style performance modeling, performance analysis; clusters: C1, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Analytical performance model for arbitrary VLA/inference-system combinations used to study how model scale, architecture, video context, async inference, dual systems and on-device/edge/cloud placement affect end-to-end latency; distills 15 takeaways.
- **[IMLE-VLA: Fast Single-Step Action Generation for Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10915)** — Kian Hosseinkhani, Qinhe Peng, George Shramko, Mehran Aghabozorgi et al. · 2026 · IROS 2026 · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: single-step generation, conditional IMLE; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: IMLE-VLA replaces the multi-step flow/diffusion action head of pi0.5 with a one-step generator trained via conditional implicit maximum likelihood estimation, keeping multimodality while removing iterative sampling.
  - Efficiency: 55 Hz vs 15 Hz inference (3.67x); up to 11x action throughput | 55 Hz vs 15 Hz inference frequency (3.67x); 3.9-6.6x lower per-episode VLA inference time (closed-loop control)
- **[Jetson-PI: Towards Onboard Real-Time Robot Control via Foresight-Aligned Asynchronous Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12659)** — Zebin Yang, Qi Wang, Yunhe Wang, Xiurui Guo et al. · 2026 · CoRL 2026 · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: asynchronous inference, future-state prediction, CUDA graph reuse, flow unrolling, future correction module, scheduling, future-state correction, GPU-resident buffering, adaptive scheduling, CUDA graphs, foresight-aligned async inference; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Combines a learned foresight module (predicting future scene features given committed actions) with confidence-based VLM/action-expert scheduling and CUDA-level system optimizations to run VLAs asynchronously on Jetson Orin.
  - Efficiency: 8.66x (vs PyTorch) and 5.41x (vs vla.cpp) higher control frequency | 8.66x / 5.41x control-frequency improvement vs naive PyTorch / vla.cpp (closed-loop control) on NVIDIA Jetson Orin
  - Code: https://github.com/PKU-SEC-Lab/Jetson-PI
- **[Latency-Tolerant Cloud-Edge Collaborative Vision-Language-Action Models via Emergent Representational Specialization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00569)** — Daojie Peng, Fulong Ma, Bingtao Wang, Sheng Wang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: cloud-edge split, delay-robust training, cloud-edge collaboration, hierarchical cloud/edge policy, delay-augmented training, asynchronous inference, latency-tolerant training; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C5, C6, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: CloudEdgeVLA trains a cloud VLA to emit slowly varying task features from delayed frames while a light edge head fuses the latest feature with current vision, keeping control robust to network delay.
  - Efficiency: 63.8-78.0% success under 40-step delay window | 63.8-78.0% success with 40-step uniform delay window (VLASH <=6.4%) (other)
- **[Learning to Act While Waiting: RL Finetuning of Generalist Robot Policies Under Inference Latency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23831)** — Brian Zhu, Momen Khalil, E Harrison, Emanuele Poggi et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: asynchronous inference, RL fine-tuning, latency-aware RL, state augmentation, RL finetuning, state augmentation with committed actions, mid-inference observation; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C6, C7
  - TL;DR: Shows inference latency breaks the Markov assumption for RL finetuning of VLAs and restores it by augmenting state with committed actions and a mid-inference observation for a low-latency RL policy.
- **[MINERVA: How Small Can a Manipulation Policy Be and Still Solve LIBERO?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03715)** — Kohei Sendai, Tatsuya Matsushima, Yusuke Iwasawa · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: minimal model design, small model, L1 regression action head, action chunking; clusters: C1, C2, C4
  - TL;DR: A 0.54M-parameter visuomotor policy reaches 95.1% average on the four LIBERO suites, showing the benchmark needs little capacity and that task-ID conditioning mostly selects memorized tasks; robustness collapses on LIBERO-Plus.
  - Efficiency: 95.1% LIBERO with 0.54M params (7,700x fewer than pi0.5) | 5-9 ms per chunk (113x faster than SmolVLA, 1400x faster than pi0.5) (relative latency) on laptop CPU
- **[MotionWAM: Towards Foundation World Action Models for Real-Time Humanoid Loco-Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09215)** — Jia Zheng, Teli Ma, Yudong Fan, Zifan Wang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: policy conditioned on intermediate video-denoising features, unified whole-body motion tokens, condition on intermediate denoising features, unified motion latent; clusters: C1, C7, C8, C9
  - TL;DR: Real-time WAM for humanoid whole-body loco-manipulation from one egocentric camera, reading intermediate video-denoising features instead of finished video and predicting unified whole-body motion tokens.
- **[Offline Semantic Guidance for Efficient Vision-Language-Action Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16241)** — Jin Shi, Brady Zhang, Yishun Lu · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: distillation, knowledge distillation; clusters: C1, C2
  - TL;DR: Distills OpenVLA (or pi0.5) into a 158M student using teacher actions plus VLM-generated phase and direction hints used only at training time.
  - Efficiency: 12.5 Hz; 3.28x speedup over OpenVLA-7B; 44x smaller (throughput) on RTX 4090
- **[OpenPi π₀.₅ on Jetson Thor](https://www.jetson-ai-lab.com/tutorials/openpi_on_thor/)** — Aditya Sahu, Anqi Liu · 2026 · Jetson AI Lab (NVIDIA) tutorial · blog
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: FP8 quantization, NVFP4 quantization, TensorRT, TensorRT compilation; clusters: C1, C2, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Step-by-step NVIDIA tutorial deploying pi0.5 on Jetson Thor with TensorRT FP8 and NVFP4, cutting per-inference latency ~2.7x at action horizon 10.
  - Efficiency: ~132 ms (BF16 PyTorch) -> ~54 ms (TRT FP8) -> ~49 ms (TRT FP8+NVFP4), ~2.7x (open-loop chunk rate) on Jetson AGX Thor (MAXN, JetPack 7.2)
- **[OxyGen: Unified KV Cache Management for Vision-Language-Action Models under Multi-Task Parallelism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14371)** — Xiangyu Li, Huaizhi Tang, Xin Ding, Weijun Wang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: KV cache sharing, continuous batching, cross-task KV sharing, cross-frame continuous batching, unified KV cache management, unified KV cache sharing, KV cache management; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Treats the KV cache as a shared resource across concurrent VLA tasks (acting, talking, memory) and across frames, avoiding redundant prefill and decoupling language decoding from fixed-rate action generation.
  - Efficiency: up to 3.7x speedup; >200 tok/s language + 70 Hz action simultaneously | up to 3.7x over isolated execution; >200 tokens/s language + 70 Hz action frequency simultaneously (throughput) on RTX 4090; Jetson AGX Thor
- **[PredActor: Predictive Action Diffusion for Steerable Onboard Humanoid Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24840)** — Lei Ye, Hai-Bo Gao, Yi-Tang Li, Peng Xu et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: rolling denoising, runtime optimization; clusters: C1, C5
  - TL;DR: Predictive state-action diffusion policy for humanoids, optimized to run within the control period on an onboard Jetson and deployed on a Unitree G1.
  - Efficiency: 16.79 ms median / 19.38 ms p95 callback (< 20 ms control period) (closed-loop control) on Jetson Orin NX
- **[Predict Before You Deploy: Offline Prediction of Quantization-Induced Task Degradation for World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19441)** — Jiuyi Xu, Jinjia Guo, Meida Chen, Jing Du et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: post-training quantization, deployment validation, offline action-deviation screening, policy-calibrated thresholds on offline action deviation; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C8
  - TL;DR: Predicts whether a quantization configuration will hurt closed-loop task success from offline action deviations, calibrated on a small development set; shows bit width alone does not explain task loss across five WAMs.
  - Efficiency: 21/28 held-out configs decided offline, all correct (other)
  - Code: https://github.com/jiuyixu25/PreDE
- **[PRISM: Performer RS-IMLE for Single-pass Multisensory Imitation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02396)** — Amisha Bhaskar, Pratap Tokekar, S. di Cairano, Alexander V. Schperberg · 2026 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: single-pass IMLE, Performer linear attention; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: PRISM is a single-pass IMLE policy with linear attention, avoiding iterative sampling while fusing many sensors.
  - Efficiency: 30-50 Hz closed-loop control (closed-loop control)
- **[ProbeFlow: Training-Free Adaptive Flow Matching for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17850)** — Zhou Fang, Jiaqi Wang, Yi Zhou, Qiongfeng Shi et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: adaptive ODE steps, training-free acceleration, adaptive ODE step scheduling, training-free; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Training-free adaptive step scheduling for flow-matching action heads based on cosine similarity of initial and lookahead velocities.
  - Efficiency: 14.8x faster action decoding (50 -> 2.6 steps), 2.8x lower end-to-end latency on MetaWorld (relative latency)
- **[RAPID: Redundancy-Aware and Compatibility-Optimal Edge-Cloud Partitioned Inference for Diverse VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07949)** — Zihao Zheng, Sicheng Tian, Hangyu Cao, Chenyue Li et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: edge-cloud split, redundancy-aware partitioning, split computing; clusters: C1, C3, C5, C6
  - TL;DR: Edge-cloud partitioned VLA inference that avoids visual-noise-sensitive partitioning and exploits step-to-step redundancy in embodied tasks to decide what runs where.
  - Efficiency: up to 1.73x speedup with 5-7% overhead (relative latency)
- **[Realtime-VLA FLASH: Speculative Inference Framework for Diffusion-based VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13778)** — Jiahui Niu, Kefan Gu, Yucheng Zhao, Shengwen Liang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: speculative inference, speculative drafting + verification, fallback to full inference; clusters: C1, C3, C6, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Replaces most full diffusion-VLA replanning calls with a light draft model verified in parallel by the action expert, with a phase-aware fallback to full inference.
  - Efficiency: task-level avg inference latency 19.1 ms vs 58.0 ms full (3.04x) (relative latency)
- **[Reflex: Enabling Fast and Predictive Vision-Language-Action Models for Reaction-Critical Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14379)** — Yuxuan Chen, Wanruo Zhang, Xiao Li · 2026 · arXiv · benchmark
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: CUDA graph replay, latent future prediction, batched visual encoding, benchmark with configurable latency; clusters: C1, C2, C4, C5, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Benchmark of six dynamic tasks whose evaluator decouples simulator stepping from control and injects configurable latency under sync and async inference, plus an efficient reaction-oriented VLA.
- **[Reflex: Real-Time VLA Control through Streaming Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14695)** — Yuanchun Guo, Bingyan Liu · 2026 · ICML 2026 · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: incremental KV cache, asynchronous pipeline, kernel fusion, incremental KV caching, async pipeline, operator fusion, incremental KV cache partitioning, AdaRMSNorm; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C5, C7
  - TL;DR: Streaming inference for flow-matching VLAs using timestep-invariant perception caching, a flow-phase-gated normalization for bf16 stability, and an asynchronous encode/generate pipeline.
  - Efficiency: 2.58x speedup; 50 Hz stable streaming; up to 54% lower reaction latency | 2.58x inference speedup; 50 Hz stable streaming; up to 54% lower reaction latency (open-loop chunk rate)
- **[RhinoVLA Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07383)** — Huixi Technology, :, Chen Zhang, Chenyang Zhou et al. · 2026 · arXiv · tech-report
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: hardware co-design, mixed precision, token reduction, compilation, token-efficient backbone, hardware-aware compilation, parallel visual encoding, LoRA; clusters: C1, C5
  - TL;DR: Deployment-oriented VLA co-designed with the Huixi R1 edge SoC: token-efficient Qwen3-VL backbone, continuous action expert, hardware-aware compilation, mixed precision and parallel visual encoding.
  - Efficiency: 11.69 Hz end-to-end inference (closed-loop control) on Huixi R1 edge SoC
  - Code: https://github.com/HuixiAI/RhinoVLA
- **[RLRC: Reinforcement Learning-based Recovery for Compressed Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17639)** — Yuxuan Chen, Yixin Han, Yize Huang, Xiao Li · 2026 · IEEE RA-L 2026 · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: structured pruning, SFT recovery, RL fine-tuning, quantization, pruning, SFT+RL recovery, SFT, RL recovery, RL-based recovery; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C4, C9
  - TL;DR: Systematically studies VLA compression and proposes prune -> SFT+RL recovery -> quantize, using RL with critic warm-up and BC regularization to regain success rate lost to pruning.
  - Efficiency: up to 8x memory reduction, 2.3x inference speedup (relative memory)
- **[Shallow-π: Knowledge Distillation for Flow-based VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20262)** — Boseong Jeon, Yunho Choi, Taehan Kim, Yun-Ho Choi · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: distillation, depth reduction, knowledge distillation, depth reduction 18->6 layers, depth reduction of backbone and action head; clusters: C1, C2, C3, C5, C9
  - TL;DR: Distills both the VLM backbone and flow action head of a pi-style VLA from 18 to 6 layers and validates onboard on Jetson Orin and Thor across robots including humanoids.
  - Efficiency: >2x faster inference, 18->6 layers, <1% success drop (relative latency) on Jetson Orin, Jetson Thor
- **[SnapFlow: One-Step Action Generation for Flow-Matching VLAs via Progressive Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05656)** — Wuyang Luan, Junhui Li, Weiguang Zhao, Wenjian Zhang et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: one-step generation, self-distillation, step distillation, consistency training; clusters: C1, C2
  - TL;DR: Self-distills flow-matching VLAs to one function evaluation by mixing flow targets with two-step Euler shortcut targets, with no teacher or architecture change.
  - Efficiency: end-to-end latency 274 ms -> 83 ms (pi0.5); 9.6x denoising speedup (relative latency)
- **[STEP: Warm-Started Visuomotor Policies with Spatiotemporal Consistency Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08245)** — Jin-Hao Li, Yuxuan Cong, Ying-Qiao Wang, Hao Xia et al. · 2026 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: warm-start, consistency prediction; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: STEP builds spatiotemporally consistent warm-start actions so diffusion policies need very few denoising steps while keeping quality.
- **[The Gate, Not the Cache: Gate Provenance Bounds the Closed-Loop Reliability of Training-Free VLA Token Skipping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00391)** — Qi Luo, Shuaijun Liu, Hao Zhao, Kunlin Li et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: token skipping, KV cache reuse, token pruning; clusters: C1, C4
  - TL;DR: Shows training-free token skipping compounds errors across control steps when gates come from previous accelerated passes, a failure invisible to action-level detectors; a dense refresh during chunk execution restores success.
  - Efficiency: success 0.68 (reuse) / 0.31 (deletion) vs dense 1.00 at skip ratio 0.9; recovers to 0.98 with refresh (other)
- **[The Speedup Paradox: Rethinking Inference Speed-Quality Trade-off in Embodied Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28529)** — Yujin Wang, Junli Chen, Yixuan Li, Shunan Dong et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: analysis framework, analysis of quantization, pruning, async inference, quantization, pruning, asynchronous inference, analytical decomposition (TISED); clusters: C1, C2, C4, C5, C7, C9
  - TL;DR: Analytical framework decomposing how lossy inference optimizations affect task-level outcomes: on static tasks they can lengthen completion time despite lower per-step latency; on dynamic tasks moderate lossiness can raise success above baseline; sweet spots shift with hardware.
- **[Towards Efficient Embodied Reasoning: Mixture-of-Depth Compute Allocation for Vision-Language-Action Model](https://doi.org/10.1145/3770855.3817651)** — Weiying Xie, Qingcheng Zeng, Zihan Meng, Jiayu Tian et al. · 2026 · Proceedings of the 32nd ACM SIGKDD Conference on Knowledge Discovery and Data Mining V.2 · paper
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: mixture-of-depth, token-wise depth allocation; clusters: C1, C5
  - TL;DR: MoDeVLA allocates network depth per token based on an action-aligned attribution analysis, cutting VLA compute while preserving control-relevant features.
  - Efficiency: ~38% lower latency, 86% fewer FLOPs (relative latency) on NVIDIA Jetson Orin
- **[TurboVLA: Real-Time Vision-Language-Action Model at 32 Hz on an RTX 4090 with <1 GB VRAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27205)** — Hengyi Xie, Chenfei Yao, Xianjin Wu, Yingying Zhu et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 5/5; techniques: LLM-free architecture, small backbone, compact architecture; clusters: C1, C2, C4, C9
  - TL;DR: Drops the LLM-centric pathway, fusing separately encoded vision and language directly into a compact action decoder; 0.2B model reaches 97.6% on LIBERO.
  - Efficiency: 31.2 ms latency (~32 Hz), 0.9 GiB VRAM | 31.2 ms latency, 0.9 GiB VRAM, 97.6% LIBERO (open-loop chunk rate) on RTX 4090

### Relevance 3

- **[$\tau_0$-VLA: a Hierarchical Robot Foundation Model with World-Model-Guided Test-Time Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16885)** — Xiaowei Cai, Yunuo Cai, Bing Chen, Jingxi Chen et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint

- **[$N_0$-TWAM: Scaling Tactile-Native World-Action Model for Contact-Rich Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23783)** — NeoteAI Team, Fudan TEAI Team · 2026 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 3/5; techniques: asymmetric mixture of transformers, asymmetric mixture-of-transformers; clusters: C1, C8
  - TL;DR: Large-scale tactile-native world-action model predicting future vision and contact; uses an asymmetric Mixture-of-Transformers with slim action/tactile experts for real-time efficiency.
- **[A hierarchical approach to imitation learning for manipulation tasks requiring time varying forces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03103)** — Rishabh Shukla, Adithya Santhosh, Shaili Gandhi, Samrudh Moode et al. · 2026 · Robotics Comput. Integr. Manuf. · paper
  - relevance 3/5; techniques: hierarchical control, neural impedance controller; clusters: C1, C7

- **[A Latency-Aware Framework for Visuomotor Policy Learning on Industrial Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14255)** — Daniel Ruan, Salma Mozaffari, Sigrid Adriaenssens, Arash Adel · 2026 · arXiv · preprint

  - TL;DR: Timestamps predicted action sequences and executes only temporally feasible actions to handle observation/inference/execution latency on industrial arms.

- **[Accelerating Structured Chain-of-Thought in Autonomous Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02864)** — Yi Gu, Yan Wang, Yuxiao Chen, Yurong You et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 3/5; techniques: parallel decoding, structured CoT; clusters: C1

- **[Act, Think or Abstain: Complexity-Aware Adaptive Inference for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05147)** — Riccardo Andrea Izzo, Gianluca Bardaro, Matteo Matteucci · 2026 · arXiv · preprint

- **[Action Draft and Verify: A Self-Verifying Framework for Vision-Language-Action Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18091)** — Chen Zhao, Zhuoran Wang, Haoyang Li, Shifeng Bao et al. · 2026 · arXiv · preprint

- **[Action Map Policy: Learning 3D Closed-loop Manipulation via Pixel Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10706)** — Hao-zhe Huang, Zhang-Chen Ye, Lin-Feng Zhao, Bo-Ce Hu et al. · 2026 · arXiv.org · preprint

- **[Universal Actions for Enhanced Embodied Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10105)** — Jinliang Zheng, Jianxiong Li, Dong-Xiu Liu, Yinan Zheng et al. · 2025 · Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: universal action space; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: UniAct learns a shared tokenized universal action space across heterogeneous embodiments, enabling a small embodied foundation model that decodes to robot-specific actions.
- **[villa-X: Enhancing Latent Action Modeling in Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23682)** — Xiaoyu Chen, Hangxing Wei, Pushi Zhang, Chu-Heng Zhang et al. · 2025 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: latent actions; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: villa-X improves latent-action learning and integrates it into VLA pretraining for better manipulation.
- **[Vision Language Action Models in Robotic Manipulation: A Systematic Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10672)** — Muhayy Ud Din, Waseem Akram, L. S. Saoud, Jan Rosell et al. · 2025 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1, C4
  - TL;DR: Systematic review of ~100 VLA models, their datasets and simulation platforms with a taxonomy of architectures.
- **[VLA-0: Building State-of-the-Art VLAs with Zero Modification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13054)** — Ankit Goyal, Hugo Hadfield, Xu-Ning Yang, Valts Blukis et al. · 2025 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: actions as text; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: VLA-0 shows that simply emitting actions as text from an unmodified VLM, with a careful training recipe, yields a state-of-the-art VLA without new tokens or action heads.
- **[VLA-OS: Structuring and Dissecting Planning Representations and Paradigms in Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17561)** — Chongkai Gao, Zixuan Liu, Zhenghao Chi, Jun-Shan Huang et al. · 2025 · Neural Information Processing Systems · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1, C4
  - TL;DR: Controlled study of planning paradigms and representations inside VLAs across many benchmarks.
- **[WorldVLA: Towards Autoregressive Action World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21539)** — Jun Cen, Chaohui Yu, Hangjie Yuan, Yuming Jiang et al. · 2025 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1, C8
  - TL;DR: Unifies a VLA policy and an image-predicting world model inside one autoregressive model so action and future-frame generation reinforce each other.
- **[X-VLA: Soft-Prompted Transformer as Scalable Cross-Embodiment Vision-Language-Action Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10274)** — Jinliang Zheng, Jianxiong Li, Zhihao Wang, Dong-Xiu Liu et al. · 2025 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: soft prompting, flow matching; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Cross-embodiment flow-matching VLA that handles heterogeneous robot data with per-source learnable soft-prompt embeddings on a plain Transformer encoder; a 0.9B model reaches strong results across many sim and real benchmarks.
- **[3D Diffuser Actor: Policy Diffusion with 3D Scene Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10885)** — Tsung-Wei Ke, Nikolaos Gkanatsios, Katerina Fragkiadaki · 2024 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: 3D denoising transformer; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Diffusion policy with a 3D relative-position denoising transformer over scene features; SOTA on RLBench and CALVIN.
- **[A Self-Correcting Vision-Language-Action Model for Fast and Slow System Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17418)** — Chenxuan Li, Jiaming Liu, Guanqun Wang, Xiaoqi Li et al. · 2024 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: dual-system architecture; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Single VLA with a fast direct pose-prediction mode and a slow chain-of-thought reflection mode invoked after failures.
- **[BAKU: An Efficient Transformer for Multi-Task Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07539)** — Siddhant Haldar, Zhuoran Peng, Lerrel Pinto · 2024 · Neural Information Processing Systems · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: action chunking, multi-task transformer; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Simple multi-task transformer combining observation trunks, action chunking and action heads for data-efficient policy learning.
- **[Fit and Prune: Fast and Training-free Visual Token Pruning for Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10197)** — Wei-Hao Ye, Qiong Wu, Wenhao Lin, Yi-Yi Zhou · 2024 · AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: visual token pruning, training-free; clusters: C1; foundational-technique
  - TL;DR: Fit and Prune is a training-free visual token pruning method for multimodal LLMs that fits a pruning recipe to a compute budget from attention statistics.
- **[GR-2: A Generative Video-Language-Action Model with Web-Scale Knowledge for Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06158)** — Chi-Lam Cheang, Guangzeng Chen, Ya Jing, Tao Kong et al. · 2024 · arXiv · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: video pretraining; clusters: C1, C8
  - TL;DR: Generalist manipulation agent pretrained on tens of millions of internet video clips, then fine-tuned for joint video generation and action prediction.
- **[LLARVA: Vision-Action Instruction Tuning Enhances Robot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11815)** — Dantong Niu, Yuvan Sharma, Giscard Biamby, Jerome Quenum et al. · 2024 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: instruction tuning; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: LLARVA instruction-tunes a multimodal model on vision-action data with 2D visual traces as auxiliary targets for robot learning.
- **[LLaVA-Prumerge: Adaptive Token Reduction for Efficient Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15388)** — Yuzhang Shang, Mu Cai, Bing-Xin Xu, Yong Jae Lee et al. · 2024 · IEEE International Conference on Computer Vision · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: visual token reduction; clusters: C1; foundational-technique
  - TL;DR: PruMerge adaptively selects and merges visual tokens in multimodal LLMs based on attention sparsity, greatly reducing token count with little accuracy loss.
- **[Moto: Latent Motion Token as the Bridging Language for Learning Robot Manipulation from Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04445)** — Yi Chen, Yu-Ying Ge, Weiliang Tang, Yi-Zhuo Li et al. · 2024 · IEEE International Conference on Computer Vision · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: latent motion tokens, autoregressive pretraining; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Learns discrete latent motion tokens from videos, pretrains an autoregressive model over them, then maps to robot actions.
- **[Predictive Inverse Dynamics Models are Scalable Learners for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15109)** — Yang Tian, Si-Zhe Yang, Jia Zeng, Ping Wang et al. · 2024 · International Conference on Learning Representations · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: predictive inverse dynamics; clusters: C1, C8
  - TL;DR: Seer: end-to-end policy that predicts future visual states and infers actions via inverse dynamics, strong on LIBERO and CALVIN.
- **[Robotic Control via Embodied Chain-of-Thought Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08693)** — Michał Zawalski, William Chen, Karl Pertsch, Oier Mees et al. · 2024 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: chain-of-thought; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Trains VLAs to produce grounded multi-step reasoning (plans, subtasks, object boxes) before acting, improving generalization.
- **[Scaling Cross-Embodied Learning: One Policy for Manipulation, Navigation, Locomotion and Aviation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11812)** — Ria Doshi, H. Walke, Oier Mees, S. Dasari et al. · 2024 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: cross-embodiment transformer; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Transformer policy trained on 900K trajectories from 20 embodiments that controls arms, wheeled robots, quadrupeds and drones with one set of weights.
- **[TraceVLA: Visual Trace Prompting Enhances Spatial-Temporal Awareness for Generalist Robotic Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10345)** — Ruijie Zheng, Yongyuan Liang, Shuaiyi Huang, Jianfeng Gao et al. · 2024 · International Conference on Learning Representations · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: visual trace prompting; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: TraceVLA overlays visual traces of past motion on the image input so a VLA gains spatio-temporal awareness, improving manipulation over OpenVLA.
- **[What matters in building vision–language–action models for generalist robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14058)** — Xing-Hang Li, Pei-Yan Li, Long Qian, Minghuan Liu et al. · 2024 · Nature Machine Intelligence · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1, C4
  - TL;DR: Large empirical study of which design choices matter when turning VLMs into VLAs (backbone, action formulation, history).
- **[Any-point Trajectory Modeling for Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00025)** — Chuan Wen, Xing-Yu Lin, John So, Kai Chen et al. · 2023 · Robotics: Science and Systems Conference · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Predicts future trajectories of arbitrary points from video as an intermediate to learn policies with few action labels.
- **[MUTEX: Learning Unified Policies from Multimodal Task Specifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.14320)** — Rutav Shah, Roberto Mart'in-Mart'in, Yuke Zhu · 2023 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Unified policy that follows tasks specified via speech, text, images or video.
- **[PaLM-E: An Embodied Multimodal Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.03378)** — Danny Driess, F. Xia, Mehdi S. M. Sajjadi, Corey Lynch et al. · 2023 · International Conference on Machine Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Google's embodied multimodal language model that injects continuous sensor/state embeddings into a large LLM for robot planning and VQA; a large-scale reference point efficiency work contrasts against.
- **[RoboAgent: Generalization and Efficiency in Robot Manipulation via Semantic Augmentations and Action Chunking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.01918)** — Homanga Bharadhwaj, Jay Vakil, Mohit Sharma, Abhi Gupta et al. · 2023 · IEEE International Conference on Robotics and Automation · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: action chunking, semantic augmentation; clusters: C1, C3
  - TL;DR: RoboAgent trains a multi-task manipulation agent from a small dataset using generative semantic augmentation and action-chunked transformer policies.
- **[Diffusion Policies as an Expressive Policy Class for Offline Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.06193)** — Zhen-Dong Wang, Jonathan J. Hunt, Ming-Yuan Zhou · 2022 · International Conference on Learning Representations · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Represents offline-RL policies as diffusion models combined with Q-learning, showing expressive policy classes help offline RL.
- **[Do As I Can, Not As I Say: Grounding Language in Robotic Affordances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.01691)** — Brian Ichter, Anthony Brohan, Yevgen Chebotar, Chelsea Finn et al. · 2022 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Grounds LLM task planning in robot affordance value functions so a language model only proposes skills the robot can actually execute.
- **[Fast Inference from Transformers via Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.17192)** — Yaniv Leviathan, Matan Kalman, Yossi Matias · 2022 · International Conference on Machine Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; techniques: speculative decoding; clusters: C1; foundational-technique
  - TL;DR: Introduces speculative decoding: a small draft model proposes several tokens that the large model verifies in parallel, giving exact-distribution speedups for autoregressive transformers.
- **[Perceiver-Actor: A Multi-Task Transformer for Robotic Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05451)** — Mohit Shridhar, Lucas Manuelli, D. Fox · 2022 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Perceiver-based multi-task agent that encodes RGB-D voxels and language to predict next-best 6-DoF keyframe actions.
- **[R3M: A Universal Visual Representation for Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.12601)** — Suraj Nair, A. Rajeswaran, Vikash Kumar, Chelsea Finn et al. · 2022 · Conference on Robot Learning · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Pretrains a compact visual representation on human egocentric video for data-efficient downstream robot manipulation as a frozen perception module.
- **[VIMA: General Robot Manipulation with Multimodal Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03094)** — Yunfan Jiang, Agrim Gupta, Zichen Zhang, Guanzhi Wang et al. · 2022 · arXiv.org · preprint
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1, C4
  - TL;DR: Transformer agent that takes interleaved text/image prompts for many manipulation tasks, with an accompanying benchmark.
- **[Language Conditioned Imitation Learning Over Unstructured Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.07648)** — Corey Lynch, P. Sermanet · 2020 · Robotics: Science and Systems Conference · paper
  - relevance 2/5; clusters: C1
  - TL;DR: Learns language-conditioned policies from unstructured play data with a small fraction of language labels.
